# Basilius Valentinus

...I cannot dissuade anyone from the logic of the <term>Great Stone</term> <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the ultimate goal of alchemy, capable of transmuting base metals and healing the human body.</note>—if I wish to convert metals with profit—as being useful for any stone: for although in the beginning a physical form, and a corporeal, visible, and tangible entity is taken up, nonetheless, from that formal body, a certain spiritual and celestial entity must be elicited. Should I call it an "appearance"? For no other name occurs to me by which I might call it. This entity was previously infused into that body by the stars, and perfected and ripened by the elements. Moreover, this spiritual entity must again be brought back to a tangible, fixed, solid, and indestructible matter through a lesser fire, and by the governance and direction of the <term>microcosm</term> <note>The alchemist and his laboratory environment are viewed as a "little world" (microcosm) that mirrors the workings of the Great Universe (macrocosm).</note>.

But what am I doing, or what am I saying? It is as if beyond myself, reason—

This mystery is communicated by its Author—that is, <term>nature-naturing<note>Latin: *natura naturans*; a philosophical concept referring to Nature as an active, creative force or God's agency within the world, as opposed to the mere physical objects of the world.</note></term>—through intellectual revelation only to the worthy and chosen "sons of the art." ### Basilius Valentinus. 322

and [my] hands, I would command that they proceed no further in writing. <note>The author concludes his thought from the previous page: even if he tried to stop himself from revealing these secrets, his desire to help would prevent him from commanding his own hands to stop writing.</note>

<margin>A love for metals must be implanted in tinctures.</margin>
All **tinctures** <term>tincture<note>In alchemy, a tincture is a concentrated substance or "spiritual" essence capable of transforming or "dyeing" a base metal into a noble one (like gold), or healing the human body.</note></term> of metals must be prepared in such a way that they are carried toward the metals by a certain special love, and possess an inclination and a desire to unite themselves with them and to bring them back into a better state. Do you wish for an emblem, or an example? Look to it in human beings. If any two are inflamed by mutual love, there is no delay found, nor rest, until they are united and satisfy their desire; after this union, they rest and are multiplied according to the good pleasure of God and the promise of blessing. <note>Alchemists often used the metaphor of human marriage and procreation to describe "chemical affinity"—the natural attraction and reaction between different substances.</note>

...and [human] strengths are restored. But love— <margin>He speaks at length regarding love.</margin> —is a disease with which no other sickness can be compared. Natural diseases are usually tempered according to a person’s <term>complexion<note>In early medicine, the 'complexion' was the specific balance of the four humors—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—which determined an individual's temperament and physical health.</note></term>; consequently, they strike some people more fiercely and others more mildly. <margin>The differences between various fires <note>original: "Ignium variorum differentiæ"</note>.</margin> The first is celestial fire, kindled by God in our hearts, by which we are carried by a certain inflamed love and sure confidence in the true God our Creator, in the most holy and incomprehensible Trinity <note>original: "sanctissimam & incomprehensibilem Trinitatem"</note>, and in the mercy and grace of our Savior Jesus Christ. This confidence, kindled by love, will never fail us, nor will it ever desert us in our times of need, but will most certainly free our souls from eternal destruction.

The second is elemental fire, produced by the sun, and it tends toward this purpose: that it may lead all things to maturity within the macrocosm <term>macrocosm: the "great world" or the universe; alchemists believed the same laws governed the stars as governed the laboratory</term>.

The third is corporal <note>physical or tangible</note> fire, by which all foods and medicines are cooked—

# The Triumph of Antimony

...[the final] cooking and ripening is achieved, so that man can use and enjoy that gift of the gods for his own sustenance, through which the last and least thing—produced from the first and greatest—can complete its own work.

The true oil of antimony, from which the so-called **Stone of Fire** <note>original: "lapis ignis," a potent alchemical substance believed to possess great medicinal and transmutative power</note> is made, is exceedingly sweet. It is purified and separated from its "earth" <note>the "earth" refers to the solid, impure residue left after distillation or extraction</note> in such a way that if a glass vessel full of it is exposed to the sun, it casts various and wonderful rays, as if many fiery mirrors were present in the room, reflecting the colors of fiery bronze <term>pyropum: a brilliant gold-bronze alloy or a deep red gemstone; here describing a radiant, shimmering light</term> and other hues. <insert>253</insert>

*...and from it is made a red, dry, fixed, and flowing* <note>original: fluidus</note> *powder, which will not give off even the slightest bit of smoke.*

*Now I shall proceed, my apostle and disciple of secrets, and I shall speak in the manner of a Prophet. When you have brought your Philosophical studies to this end, in the manner I have prescribed for you, you possess the medicine of men and of metals; it is pleasant and sweet in use, beyond all danger, it is penetrating, and does not cause any*

---

*\* Stand far off, stand far off, you uninitiated* <note>This is a reference to Virgil's Aeneid, warning the "profane" or unlearned to stay away from sacred mysteries</note>*, and you, who are initiated into the sacred Chemical* <note>original: Chymicis</note> *arts, favor this work with your hearts and tongues. The King enters into his bridal chamber to consume the marriage. O, with what great things your glory shall lift you up by such a union! Yet do not hasten too much to enter, or to disturb this marital union; for many months they shall delight themselves together in mutual embraces, nor shall they emerge, until out of mutual love they have been changed into a hermaphroditic body, and have brought forth that son desired by all—if not a King of Kings, then at least a Little King* <term>Regulus: A technical term for the metallic form of antimony, but used here as a pun for a "little king" or prince</term>*, who may relieve his subjects of diseases and poverty.*

<meta>catchword: se-</meta>

<summary>This page describes the final state of the antimonial powder—a stable, red substance—and transitions into highly allegorical language describing the "alchemical marriage" of substances. This union produces the 'Regulus,' a potent medicinal and transformative agent that heals both the human body and the state of poverty.</summary>

<keywords>Triumph of Antimony, Philosophical Medicine, Alchemical Marriage, Regulus, Hermaphroditic body, transmutation, healing</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Little King" or Regulus of Antimony, describing the medicinal and material benefits of the perfected substance, while the author expresses a spiritual crisis regarding the disclosure of these divine secrets.</meta>

### Basilius Valentinus

...seat <note>original: sedes; referring to the physical location or base of the medicine's power</note>, it brings correction <term>emendatio: in an alchemical context, this refers to the purging of impurities and the restoration of a substance or body to its perfect state</term>, and drives out evil; use it as it is proper, and it will bring you many benefits, both for health and for your earthly needs, by which it will happen that you are beyond all want in this world. This is a matter of such great value that no sacrifice of gratitude can be found that could repay this kindness <note>original: indulgentia</note> of God toward you. At this point, O my God, as a man of religion I am distressed in spirit, for I do not know if I am doing well or wrongly, or if I am carried too far beyond the proper limits * by my speaking, or if I stop short of them; meanwhile, I set these things forth, and lead everyone as if to their own home. You, young student of this art, seek and experiment,

---

<margin>* Our Author seems to think he has said too much; if you feel the same, you will find joy in his sorrow. It is a wonder, however, that no one has brought even the smallest light to this Philosophy <note>The "Hermetic Philosophy" or alchemy</note> without immediately regretting it.</margin>

<summary>The author describes the perfected medicine as a divine gift that provides both health and wealth, but he expresses deep spiritual anxiety over whether he has revealed too many sacred secrets. An editorial note observes that alchemical authors often feel immediate regret after sharing their hidden knowledge.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the concluding exhortations of the author, often identified as the legendary alchemist Basil Valentine, regarding the proper study of antimony. The author shifts from technical instruction to a moral warning about the responsibility of the student.</meta>

# The Triumph of Antimony

...as I have done: if you reach the target <note>original: *scopum*, meaning the goal or mark of the alchemical work</note>, give the highest thanks to God, and after God to me, your <term>Teacher<gloss>The Latin *Præceptor* suggests a master who guides an initiate through the spiritual and physical stages of alchemy.</gloss></term>; but if you wander into devious or pathless ways, you do so by your own fault. Blame yourself and let me go, for I am not responsible for your error.

I have now said enough, and written enough, and taught so clearly, so openly, and so brilliantly, that it could not be made more manifest or more lucid in writing, unless someone—lost and reckless—knowingly and willingly wishes to cast themselves down into the <term>underworld<gloss>original: *inferos*. This likely refers to the spiritual or physical ruin that follows the misuse of chemical substances or the pursuit of alchemy for greed.</gloss></term>, to be submerged there and perish; because it has been most severely forbidden by the Creator of all things to uncover these secrets further, or to eat from the <note>This is an allusion to the Tree of Knowledge in the Garden of Eden; alchemists often compared their "Great Work" to the forbidden knowledge of Paradise.</note> tree that was planted in the middle of Paradise. Here, therefore, I will pause for a while, until others have indicated what they think I should do or omit in this place,

<summary>The author concludes his instruction on antimony by warning the reader that any failure is their own, as he has provided the clearest possible guidance. He invokes the biblical imagery of the Tree of Knowledge to justify why he must stop revealing certain alchemical secrets.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, antimony, Basil Valentine, Præceptor, Tree of Knowledge, Paradise, alchemical error, divine prohibition</keywords>

## Basilius Valentinus

Let us therefore cease speaking of this and pass on to its use.

Know then that the use consists in the observation of the person and their temperament <note>original: *complexionis*; in early medicine, this refers to the specific balance of humors—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—within an individual's body</note>, insofar as it pertains to human health, so that nature is not overwhelmed by an excessive quantity, nor left unaided by too small a dose. Excessive quantity, however, is not to be feared so scrupulously; for it does not easily harm, because it helps in recovering former health and resists poison <margin>Powers</margin>, if any should happen to be hidden within. 

I add only this: three or four grains, administered in a single dose, suffice to drive away every malady, when taken in spirit of wine <note>original: *spiritu vini*; this refers to distilled alcohol, which alchemists used as a potent solvent to "carry" the medicine into the bloodstream</note>; for this stone <note>original: *lapis*; here referring to the "Philosopher's Stone" or the fixed medicinal form of antimony</note> or tincture <note>original: *tinctura*; a concentrated liquid extract of the substance's essence</note> creeps through all the members of the body and contains within itself the virtues of many <term>secrets<gloss>original: *arcanorum*. In alchemical medicine, an "arcanum" is a hidden, powerful substance that can change or cure the body.</gloss></term>; it takes away dizziness of the head <note>original: *vertiginem capitis*</note> and all diseases that take their origin from the lungs; it drives away diffi-

<summary>The author provides clinical instructions on administering the "stone" or "tincture" of antimony, advising that dosage should be adjusted to the patient's temperament while assuring that the remedy is safe and effective against poisons and respiratory issues.</summary>
<keywords>dosage, temperament, spirit of wine, medicinal stone, tincture, vertigo, lung diseases, arcana</keywords>

for Basil <note>Referring to the author, Basilius Valentinus.</note> assumes that you are

[...reme]dy <note>The text begins mid-word with "dium," the conclusion of the Latin "remedium."</note> is for all symptoms which can happen to the human body, which experience will more clearly demonstrate to you and will open a further path, provided you are a physician <term>physician: from the Latin 'medicus,' implying a healer who understands both the physical and spiritual laws of nature</term> called by God to that office.

And regarding antimony <term>antimony: a metallic element that was a cornerstone of alchemical medicine, believed to have powerful purifying effects when properly prepared</term>, I have, as I believe, written enough and more than enough for my part. If anyone follows after me, let them add their own findings to these, so that the mysteries may be revealed before the completion of the world <note>original: "mundi consummationem"</note> by God's singular indulgence, for His glory and honor and for the preservation of health. With these words, I now wrap myself in silence for a time and return to the monastery <note>The author, Basil Valentine, was traditionally identified as a Benedictine monk; here he refers to returning to his cloistered life of study.</note>, so that I may there devote effort to further philosophy <term>philosophy: in this context, 'natural philosophy' or alchemy, the study of the hidden powers of nature</term>, through which I may comment on other matters. # Basilius Valentinus

...I will reveal their operations and virtues.

May God, the Lord of heaven and earth, grant us earthly health in this life, and afterward eternal salvation for the refreshment of our souls in the dwellings of joy and gladness, which shall never be ended by any bounds of time. Amen.

Thus I conclude this treatise on antimony <term>antimony: a metallic element and mineral that Valentinus championed as a supreme medicinal source when properly purified</term>, including all that I have written concerning the **red oil of antimony**—which is fashioned from highly purified sulfur—and concerning the **spirit** which is prepared from its salt <term>salt: in alchemical theory, 'salt' represents the principle of body or fixity, often the crystalline residue left after distillation</term>. 

Direct your mind to these matters, and compare them with these final things I have written for you concerning the **stone of fire** <term>stone of fire: or *Lapis Ignis*, a powerful red tincture or medicine produced through the advanced processing of antimony</term>. If you consider these things keenly, you will easily find the union through this comparison, even unto the end. For the foundation is the same, the reasoning is the same, and the same affinity <note>original: "amicitia," implying a natural friendship or chemical attraction between substances</note> through which health is acquired, and the long-sought stag <note>The "stag" is a classic alchemical allegory. It often represents the volatile spirit or the secret of the Work that the alchemist must "hunt" and capture through careful experimentation.</note> is captured in a joyful hunt.

<summary>Basilius Valentinus concludes his treatise on antimony with a blessing and a final encouragement to the reader to seek the "stone of fire" using the same principles he has laid out. He uses the metaphor of a "joyful hunt" to describe the pursuit of alchemical secrets.</summary>

<keywords>Basilius Valentinus, antimony, red oil of antimony, salt of antimony, stone of fire, alchemical allegory, the stag, health and salvation</keywords>

<meta>This page marks a transition from the alphabetical index found on the previous page to the beginning of a thematic chapter, likely Chapter 10 (C. X.). While the previous page focused on the medicinal and chemical properties of antimony, this text shifts to a philosophical discussion regarding the "powers" or faculties (sensation, movement, reason) found in physical matter.</meta>

### <unclear>Chapter 10</unclear>

->III.<-
**Mixed bodies** <note>original: *Corpora mixta*; in the natural philosophy of this period, these are substances composed of a mixture of the four elements, including minerals, plants, and animals.</note> thrive by a soul or a Spirit: not all in the same way, but some more, and others less.

->IV.<-
The **power of sensation** <note>original: *Vis sentiendi*; the faculty of perceiving the physical world through senses.</note> in these bodies does not manifest in all their organs.

->V.<-
The power of sensation exists in them according to how their organs are arranged.

->VI.<-
The **power of self-movement** <note>original: *Vis se movendi*; the ability of a being to move itself without an external force.</note> does not always follow from the power of sensation.

->VII.<-
The **power of reasoning** <note>original: *Vis ratiocinandi*; the higher intellectual faculty of logic and thought.</note> does not always follow from the power of sensation.

->VIII.<-
The power of reasoning, etc., is sharper according to the variety of the organs.

In man, these things are not lacking in every mystery of nature. Life and unity are applied everywhere toward certain ends; in so many operations, the logic is not the same for all. While one seeks from this side, yet in others, etc., the same <unclear>p...</unclear>

<meta>catchword: <unclear>v</unclear></meta>

<summary>This page presents a series of philosophical axioms regarding how "mixed bodies" (matter) are animated by spirit and soul. It explores the hierarchy of faculties, noting that sensation, movement, and reason depend on the complexity and arrangement of physical organs.</summary>

<keywords>mixed bodies, soul, spirit, power of sensation, self-movement, reason, natural philosophy, physiology</keywords>

--- Page 800 ---
<meta>This page marks a departure from the previous page's philosophical inquiry into the nature of "mixed bodies" and the faculties of sensation and reason. It shifts into a set of formal "Canons" (rules or statutes). Based on the internal vocabulary and the warning provided, these are the concluding laws of the Rosicrucian Order as presented by Michael Maier in his work 'Themis Aurea' (Laws of the Fraternity of the Rosie Cross). These canons use the metaphor of "Royal authority" to describe the completion of the alchemical Great Work and the governance of the secret society.</meta>

-> ### <unclear>CANONS</unclear> <-

20. With a **unique signet ring**, <note>original: *Annulo singulari signato*</note> which seals upon the shield of the King, as is evident from that constitution by which royal authority is usually distinguished. <note>In the context of the Rosicrucian 'laws,' this refers to the official seal or mark of the order that validates their documents and authority.</note>

21. When the **governance of the Sun and Moon** <note>original: *Solis et Lunae regimine*; in alchemical thought, the Sun (Gold/Male) and Moon (Silver/Female) must be perfectly balanced and governed to achieve the 'Philosopher's Stone' or spiritual perfection.</note> is complete, and the crown of all Kings shines forth.

22. He should see with his own **eyes** that which he might learn through others. <note>This canon emphasizes the importance of direct experience and empirical observation—seeing the truth of nature for oneself rather than relying solely on the testimony of books or other people.</note>

23. After this same reading, **outsiders** <note>original: *externi*; those not yet initiated into the secret mysteries of the fraternity</note> may be admitted, to whom the same Royal authority shall be demonstrated.

24. And then, all honoring the King, they shall sing a **concluding hymn of praise**. <note>original: *epiphonema gratulationis*; an 'epiphonema' is a striking concluding exclamation or a short summary verse intended to emphasize what has gone before.</note>
